# Requirements (languages: en • [zh](REQUIREMENTS.zh.md) • [hi](REQUIREMENTS.hi.md) • [ru](REQUIREMENTS.ru.md))

This document is the canonical, top-level list of requirements that
`meta-sovereign` is building against. It collects directives from
implemented issues and maintainer comments so later PRs can extend one
stable requirements ledger.

For iteration-by-iteration breakdowns of how requirements have been
satisfied, see the per-issue folders under
[`docs/case-studies/`](./case-studies/).
[`docs/ROADMAP.md`](./ROADMAP.md) is intentionally present as an empty
review ledger; R-J10 records the single-PR completion criterion.

Each requirement carries a stable `R-*` identifier so changesets, PRs,
and code comments can reference it.

---

## Known ID clashes

Historical numbering was never renumbered because code comments cite the
IDs. Two clashes exist; cite them with a section qualifier:

- `R-N1`..`R-N9` are defined three times: section N (tutorial), section O
  (email) and section U (mobile UI). Cite them as `R-N1 (tutorial)`,
  `R-N1 (email)`, `R-N1 (mobile UI)`. `R-N10` is defined in sections O
  (email) and U (mobile UI); `R-N11` and `R-N12` only in U.
- The issue #18 case study
  ([`docs/case-studies/issue-18/requirements.md`](./case-studies/issue-18/requirements.md))
  uses historical `R-I*` IDs that clash with section I of this file. Cite
  them as `issue-18 R-I<k>`; a bare `R-I<k>` means section I here.

All other IDs are unique (checked by script over the requirement tables).

---

## A. Data layer

| ID   | Requirement                                                                               | State                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ---- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-A1 | Import/export with the unified database via external services (live API + bulk archives). | Done: all ten source adapters parse archives or API payloads and expose live read/write surfaces where the upstream service permits it; live imports are normalized into `msg:*` links and stamped through `pullLiveInto()`. |
| R-A2 | Import/export via `.lino` files using `links-notation` + `lino-objects-codec`.            | Done: `LinoTextStore` + indented codec round-trip in JS and Rust.                                                                                                                                                            |
| R-A3 | Unified database stored simultaneously in binary (Doublets) and text (`.lino`) form.      | Done: `DualStore` keeps both in sync with `verify()`.                                                                                                                                                                        |
| R-A4 | Regular automatic backups to a configurable archive directory.                            | Done: `createBackupScheduler` (`.unref()`-ed interval), AES-256-GCM at rest; `secret:*` links are also encrypted-at-rest via `wrapSecretStore` and never leave the local node.                                               |
| R-A5 | Indented Links Notation preferred over bracketed form for human-readable data exchange.   | Done: codec emits indented form; parser accepts both.                                                                                                                                                                        |

## B. Unified communication UI

| ID   | Requirement                                                                    | State                                                                           |
| ---- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-B1 | Unified chat UI rendering chats from all supported services in one place.      | Done: `js/src/web/views.js` chat view with virtualised rendering.               |
| R-B2 | Auto-completion in the chat UI based on the user's previous outgoing messages. | Done: `/api/autocomplete` + offline-client local fallback.                      |
| R-B3 | Operator UI that auto-switches between chats/contexts (DONE/NEXT card stream). | Done: unread conversation cards with Next/Done (N/D outside fields); see R-Z28. |
| R-B4 | Unified broadcasting UI for public posting on walls/feeds across services.     | Done: broadcast composer + `/api/broadcast`.                                    |

## C. Pattern matching and reply automation

| ID   | Requirement                                                                                | State                                                                      |
| ---- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-C1 | Patterns editor — infer / construct / simplify / generalise regex (and PEG) from examples. | Done: `inferRegex`, `simplifyRegex`, `compilePeg`; UI table + form.        |
| R-C2 | Reply-message variation editor with fuzzy-search-driven extraction of typical replies.     | Done: Sørensen-Dice candidate finder; reply-variation groups.              |
| R-C3 | Automated dialog scripts as an n8n-style node graph linking patterns to reply variations.  | Done: `createGraph` + graph editor view + `runGraph`.                      |
| R-C4 | Two automation modes — fully automated and semi-automated (operator confirms / overrides). | Done: `mode: 'auto' \| 'semi'` flag on graph runs and outreach plans.      |
| R-C5 | Pattern matching across multiple messages to extract personal facts per participant.       | Done: `src/facts/` produces `question → answer` pairs grouped by answerer. |

## D. Personal CRM features

| ID   | Requirement                                                                              | State                                                                                               |
| ---- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-D1 | One place to view everything known about a contact (chats, groups, communities, facts).  | Done: `aggregateContacts` + `/api/contacts` + contact view.                                         |
| R-D2 | Cross-reference / intersect sets to define a target audience.                            | Done: `src/crm/audience.js` DSL — `network:foo AND chat:bar OR (sender:x AND fact:y) NOT kind:bot`. |
| R-D3 | Mass-personal outreach (start manual conversations with templated greeting per contact). | Done: `planOutreach` / `runOutreach` (1:1 envelopes); CLI `meta-sovereign outreach`.                |
| R-D4 | Configurable local search for people, communities, companies, messages, chats.           | Done: `/api/search` Sørensen-Dice with field/network/time filters.                                  |
| R-D5 | Profile sync across all connected services.                                              | Done: `/api/profile` enqueues per-network sync envelopes; SPA profile editor + e2e cover the flow.  |
| R-D6 | Resume sync across job-board services.                                                   | Done: `/api/resume` for hh / habr-career / superjob / linkedin.                                     |

## E. External-service connectors

| ID    | Requirement                             | State                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ----- | --------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-E1  | Native support for **VK**.              | Done: `vk-export`-style archive parser plus VK method API live connector for conversations/history, `messages.send`, and profile/status sync.                                                                                                      |
| R-E2  | Native support for **Telegram**.        | Done: Telegram Desktop archive parser handles all-chats exports and duplicate local message ids; Bot API live connector supports `getUpdates`, `sendMessage`, profile sync, `secret:telegram:*` tokens, and handled-link stamps.                   |
| R-E3  | Native support for **X**.               | Done: archive parser plus X API v2 connector for DM lookup/send, mention timeline import, public post publishing, and profile update.                                                                                                              |
| R-E4  | Native support for **WhatsApp**.        | Done: per-chat export parser plus WhatsApp Cloud API connector for webhook message ingestion, opted-in text sends, and business profile updates.                                                                                                   |
| R-E5  | Native support for **Facebook**.        | Done: download-your-data parser plus Graph/Messenger connector for page conversation import, webhook ingestion, replies, and page profile metadata updates.                                                                                        |
| R-E6  | Native support for **LinkedIn**.        | Done: data-export parser plus LinkedIn REST Posts connector for API-side post import/export and resume/profile publication.                                                                                                                        |
| R-E7  | Native support for **career.habr.com**. | Done: applications archive parser plus configurable authenticated live connector for application messages and resume sync.                                                                                                                         |
| R-E8  | Native support for **hh.ru**.           | Done: negotiations archive parser plus hh.ru API connector for negotiations, reply messages, and resume create/update.                                                                                                                             |
| R-E9  | Native support for **superjob.ru**.     | Done: vacancy-response archive parser plus SuperJob API connector for response messages and resume create/update.                                                                                                                                  |
| R-E10 | Native support for **email**.           | Done: `.eml`/mbox archive parser, JSON mail normalization, browser-direct JMAP/Gmail/Microsoft Graph live connectors, Node local-server IMAP/POP3/SMTP fallback, and local-server `/api/email/pull` + `/api/email/send` routes.                    |
| R-E11 | Native support for **Naukri**.          | Done: browser-driven CV plan (`js/src/cv/platforms/naukri.js`) reads profile, skills, employment and education and writes headline, key skills and employment through the profile editor. No public API exists; see section V.                     |
| R-E12 | Native support for **VietnamWorks**.    | Done: browser-driven CV plan (`js/src/cv/platforms/vietnamworks.js`) reads the Next.js `__NEXT_DATA__` profile blob and writes personal details, job preferences and the CV builder fields.                                                        |
| R-E13 | Native support for **TopCV**.           | Done: browser-driven CV plan (`js/src/cv/platforms/topcv.js`) reads and writes the career objective, expectations and CV document sections. TopCV answers plain HTTP clients with a challenge page, which is why the plan requires a real browser. |

## F. Distribution, sync, and deployment

| ID   | Requirement                                                                     | State                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ---- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-F1 | Every function and feature importable as an NPM library.                        | Done: `src/*` modules expose `export` surfaces; CLI re-uses them verbatim.                                                                                                  |
| R-F2 | Published NPM library ships a CLI interface.                                    | Done: `js/bin/meta-sovereign.js` covers every server feature; `lino-arguments`-style parsing.                                                                               |
| R-F3 | Published NPM library ships an Electron desktop interface.                      | Done: `js/electron/main.js` opens the local server URL, isolates the renderer through `preload.cjs`, and configures `electron-updater` when the optional peer is installed. |
| R-F4 | Published NPM library can start a local web server connecting to local storage. | Done: `meta-sovereign serve` (Node/Deno/Bun) and `meta-sovereign-rs serve` (pure-Rust).                                                                                     |
| R-F5 | All clients sync via WebRTC mapped to local storage or a configurable endpoint. | Done: `webrtc-transport.js` browser-side; `/rtc` signalling broker on both servers.                                                                                         |
| R-F6 | Docker microservice for the WebRTC server.                                      | Done: `docker/rtc.Dockerfile` + `docker-compose.yml`.                                                                                                                       |
| R-F7 | Docker microservice for the web server.                                         | Done: `docker/web.Dockerfile` + `docker-compose.yml`.                                                                                                                       |
| R-F8 | Universal Links access interface in both server and client.                     | Done: server = `DualStore`; client = `BrowserStore` (`doublets-web`/IndexedDB/localStorage/in-memory) ⊕ `OfflineClient` (server-preferred).                                 |

## G. Stack constraints

| ID   | Requirement                                                                            | State                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ---- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-G1 | Default stack: JS on server + client + React for UI + Rust + WASM for heavy workloads. | Done: the SPA shell and all views are React components bundled by `npm run build:web`; Rust/WASM pattern matching and `doublets-web` browser storage are wired into the browser stack.  |
| R-G2 | Alternative stack: server + microservice fully written in Rust.                        | Done: `meta-sovereign-server` crate is a zero-dep `std`-only HTTP + WS + WebRTC broker; SPA boots against it identically.                                                               |
| R-G3 | Cross-platform packaging (iOS, Android, Electron) via `deep-foundation/sdk`.           | Done: Electron shell plus Capacitor fallback packaging (`capacitor.config.json`, `build:mobile`, `mobile:ios`, `mobile:android`) that reuses the same React bundle for iOS and Android. |

## H. Quality, design, and process

| ID   | Requirement                                                                   | State                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ---- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-H1 | UI follows Apple HIG / Google Material / Microsoft design best practices.     | Done: `docs/UI-DESIGN-AUDIT.md` maps every current SPA surface to Apple HIG, Material, and Fluent guidance; the app uses system fonts, large tap targets, semantic landmarks, skip-link, dark-mode toggle, stateful ARIA labels, and an axe-core WCAG 2.0 A/AA e2e audit that fails on serious or critical violations. |
| R-H2 | Code is written simply; no premature optimisations.                           | Done: small React SPA components, plain `node:http` server, plain `std` Rust server.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| R-H3 | All code documented with automated API documentation generation.              | Done: `npm run docs:api` walks `js/src/` and `cargo doc --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml --no-deps --workspace` covers the Rust crates; the CI `docs-build` job rebuilds both on every push/PR and the release job attaches the tarballs to each GitHub Release.                                                        |
| R-H4 | Test coverage spans unit, integration, and e2e (`browser-commander` for e2e). | Done: the JS suite, Rust workspace tests, and real-browser e2e via `browser-commander` + Playwright in `js/tests/e2e-browser-spa.mjs` run against the JS server and (with `RUN_BROWSER_E2E_RUST=1`) the pure-Rust `meta-sovereign-rs serve` binary, asserting wire-protocol parity end-to-end.                         |
| R-H5 | CI/CD parity with the JS + Rust AI-driven-development pipeline templates.     | Done: 9-job CI matrix (Node × {Ubuntu, macOS, Windows} + Deno × 3 + Bun × 3).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| R-H6 | Project is fully open-source / public domain (Unlicense).                     | Done: `LICENSE` is Unlicense.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

## J. Maintainer directives from PR #2

The following requirements arose from comments on PR #2 and are not in
the original issue body. They are tracked here so they don't get lost
between iterations.

| ID    | Requirement                                                                                                                               | State                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ----- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| R-J1  | The SPA must work fully offline using browser storage (in-memory / `localStorage` / `IndexedDB`).                                         | Done: `pickBrowserDriver()` selects `doublets-web` when bundled, then IndexedDB, localStorage, and in-memory fallback.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| R-J2  | The SPA must autodiscover a local server on localhost or LAN, with a manual override fallback when discovery fails.                       | Done: `discover.js` probes same-origin, saved overrides, Electron/Capacitor WebView candidates from `metaSovereignShell`, localhost ports, and explicit LAN candidates; `saveServerOverride` / `clearServerOverride` keep the manual path.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| R-J3  | All apps (GitHub Pages Web, local Web, Mobile, Electron) must be able to use both **WebRTC** and **WebSocket** to reach the local server. | Done: WebSocket sync on `/ws`; WebRTC signalling on `/rtc` — both speak the same wire format on JS and Rust servers, and the Electron/Capacitor shells load the same React bundle/discovery path as local web.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| R-J4  | The data store **is** the API. Every behaviour is a handler reacting to a write.                                                          | Done: `src/handlers/` registers handlers keyed by id-prefix; the broadcast handler runs the same code path whether the write originates from the SPA, CLI, or a peer sync.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| R-J5  | Handled writes are clearly marked so when a handled link syncs to a peer, that peer does **not** re-fire the handler.                     | Done: handlers stamp `handled: { at, by }` on the link; the bus skips already-handled links.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| R-J6  | The system must remain decentralised — a browser-only deployment (e.g. on GitHub Pages) is a complete standalone database engine.         | Done: `BrowserStore` + offline-first client + WebRTC sync between two browsers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| R-J7  | All critical UI paths covered by e2e tests using [`browser-commander`](https://github.com/link-foundation/browser-commander).             | Done: `js/tests/e2e-browser-spa.mjs` drives headless Chromium via `browser-commander` + Playwright across the reachable critical UI paths (nav, write→reload, pattern infer→save, automation graph, broadcast, outreach, backup→restore, profile sync envelopes, theme, axe-core WCAG 2.0 A/AA audit, skip-link a11y, **two-browser WebRTC convergence** — page A writes via SPA `api.put`, page B observes the link in its isolated local store via the `/rtc`-brokered peer-to-peer data channel) on the JS server, and re-runs the protocol-parity subset against `meta-sovereign-rs serve` when `RUN_BROWSER_E2E_RUST=1` is set. |
| R-J8  | A full pure-Rust local server with **all protocols and features** so the SPA can connect to it instead of the Bun JS server.              | Done: `meta-sovereign-server` crate — REST (`/links`, `/sources`, `/api/*`), WebSocket sync (`/ws`), WebRTC signalling broker (`/rtc`), static SPA host, Prometheus `/metrics`, JSON access logs (`--log json`), CSP + hardening headers. 12 wire-protocol integration tests.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| R-J9  | A `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` (this file) and `docs/ROADMAP.md` listing missing features.                                                      | Done: `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` is canonical, and `docs/ROADMAP.md` is kept as an explicit empty roadmap ledger so reviewers always have a stable place to add newly discovered gaps.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| R-J10 | Plan and execute everything in a single PR (#2). Iterate until the ROADMAP is empty.                                                      | Done: PR #2 now closes the tracked roadmap; `docs/ROADMAP.md` has no open tracked items, and remaining work is normal review hardening rather than known missing requirement scope.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| R-J11 | Set reasonable timeouts at job, suite, and CI level so flaky tests fail fast and iteration stays quick.                                   | Done: every workflow job in `.github/workflows/release.yml` and `links.yml` declares `timeout-minutes` sized at ~5–10× p95 (5/10/15/30 min bands documented in `docs/BEST-PRACTICES.md` §13); `npm test` passes `--test-timeout=30000` to `node --test`; the CI bun runner passes `--timeout 30000`; the same numbers are flagged for upstreaming to the JS + Rust pipeline templates.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

## K. Hardening (issue #6)

The following requirements arose from
[issue #6](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/issues/6) —
"Improvements and hardening system from data loss or unauthorized
access". The full atomic list and traceability live in
[`docs/case-studies/issue-6/requirements.md`](./case-studies/issue-6/requirements.md);
the canonical surface is summarised below.

| ID    | Requirement                                                                                                                                              | State                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ----- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-K1  | Messages are **never** physically deleted by default — even when the upstream reports a delete; the link is marked `deleted: { at, by, reason }`.        | Done: `markDeleted` / `softDeleteLink` in `js/src/storage/soft-delete.js`; `DELETE /links/:id` tombstones by default.                                                               |
| R-K2  | Deleted links are still queryable so the user can recover them.                                                                                          | Done: `?include=tombstones` (or `?showDeleted=1`) on `GET /links` and `GET /links/:id`; `isTombstone()` helper for client filtering.                                                |
| R-K3  | Physical deletion only on **explicit operator confirmation**.                                                                                            | Done: `purgeLink(store, id, { confirm: true })` throws if not confirmed; HTTP requires both `?purge=1&confirm=1`.                                                                   |
| R-K4  | Bulk purge takes a query and refuses to run without an explicit `confirm: true` flag.                                                                    | Done: `bulkPurge(store, predicate, { confirm: true })`; `POST /api/links/purge-tombstones` requires `{ "confirm": true }` in the body and accepts `idPrefix` / `olderThan` filters. |
| R-K5  | Soft-delete propagates over sync so peers converge on the same tombstones; `purge` does **not** propagate.                                               | Done: tombstones are regular `put` writes so the existing peer mesh replicates them; `purge` calls `store.delete` directly and is therefore local-only.                             |
| R-K6  | All provider adapters report upstream deletes as soft-deletes by default.                                                                                | Done by construction: no adapter calls `store.delete` on upstream-deleted messages; the only deletion entrypoint, `DELETE /links/:id`, is now soft by default.                      |
| R-K7  | At-rest encryption uses a per-vault **master key** (data-encryption key); the master key never touches disk in plaintext.                                | Done: `js/src/storage/vault.js` generates a random 256-bit master key and stores only AES-256-GCM-wrapped copies on disk.                                                           |
| R-K8  | The master key is unlockable through **multiple methods**: passphrase, PIN, passkey (WebAuthn `prf`), authenticator-app TOTP recovery code.              | Done: `addUnlock({ kind, secret, label })` accepts `passphrase`, `pin`, `passkey`, `totp-recovery`. The browser passes `prf` blobs straight in.                                     |
| R-K9  | Adding or removing an unlock method does **not** require re-encrypting data — each method holds its own wrapped copy of the master key.                  | Done: `addUnlock` / `removeUnlock` only re-wrap the existing master key, leaving on-disk ciphertext untouched.                                                                      |
| R-K10 | The vault is loaded into memory only after a successful unlock; locking zeroes the key.                                                                  | Done: `lock()` calls `Buffer.fill(0)` on the master key and clears the in-memory reference; `encrypt`/`decrypt` throw if not unlocked.                                              |
| R-K11 | Removing the **last** unlock method is refused so users cannot accidentally orphan their data.                                                           | Done: `removeUnlock` throws when called on the only remaining method.                                                                                                               |
| R-K12 | Losing all unlock methods means the user loses access to their data and must re-import from upstream services. There is **no** recovery backdoor.        | Done: documented in `vault.js` header + this row; the wrap-the-master-key design has no escape hatch.                                                                               |
| R-K13 | Export-to-file is encrypted by default; the user is warned in the envelope that they are responsible for storing the file.                               | Done: `exportEncrypted(store, { passphrase })` wraps `encryptBackup` and embeds a top-level `warning` field; `DEFAULT_WARNING` exported for translators.                            |
| R-K14 | Encrypted exports round-trip through `decryptExport` (and `decryptBackup`) using the same passphrase.                                                    | Done: both raw `encryptBackup` envelopes and the new wrapped form open with one call.                                                                                               |
| R-K15 | The CLI exposes `meta-sovereign export-encrypted --file=… --passphrase=…`.                                                                               | Done: `js/bin/meta-sovereign.js` `export-encrypted` subcommand + `vault init/add-unlock/remove-unlock/list-unlocks` subcommands.                                                    |
| R-K16 | The HTTP server exposes `POST /api/export-encrypted` returning the same envelope, gated on a passphrase from the request body or `MS_SECRET_PASSPHRASE`. | Done: `routes-mutating.js` `handleExportEncrypted`; refuses without a passphrase.                                                                                                   |
| R-K17 | A documented `purge-tombstones` HTTP endpoint and CLI surface so operators can clean up after an audit.                                                  | Done: `POST /api/links/purge-tombstones` + `meta-sovereign purge-tombstones` both demand explicit confirmation.                                                                     |
| R-K18 | Compile data into `./docs/case-studies/issue-6/` (deep analysis, online research, requirements list, solution plans, components/libraries survey).       | Done: `docs/case-studies/issue-6/{README,requirements,solution-plan,components,external-research}.md` + raw issue/comment JSON in `data/`.                                          |
| R-K19 | Update `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` (this file) with the issue-#6 surface.                                                                                     | Done: this section.                                                                                                                                                                 |
| R-K20 | Plan and execute everything in a single PR (#7).                                                                                                         | Done: PR #7 lands the case study, soft-delete, vault, encrypted export, and CLI/HTTP surface together.                                                                              |

## L. Browser-first publishing (issue #8)

The following requirements arose from
[issue #8](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/issues/8) —
"Make sure all our features that can be supported in the browser
directly are published to GitHub Pages and make user friendly
documentation". The full atomic list and traceability live in
[`docs/case-studies/issue-8/requirements.md`](./case-studies/issue-8/requirements.md);
the canonical surface is summarised below.

| ID     | Requirement                                                                                                    | State                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-L1   | All browser-supportable features must be reachable from a public web app.                                      | Done: the GitHub Pages bundle is the same `js/src/web/` SPA that the desktop and mobile apps embed, so feature parity holds by construction.                                                 |
| R-L2   | The public web app must be hosted on GitHub Pages.                                                             | Done: `pages.yml` deploys `dist/pages/` via `actions/deploy-pages@v4`.                                                                                                                       |
| R-L3   | Publishing to GitHub Pages must happen automatically via GitHub Actions on every push to the default branch.   | Done: `pages.yml` triggers on `push: branches: [main]` and on `workflow_dispatch`.                                                                                                           |
| R-L3a  | Pages workflow must use `actions/configure-pages`, `actions/upload-pages-artifact`, `actions/deploy-pages`.    | Done: `pages.yml` pins `actions/configure-pages@v5`, `actions/upload-pages-artifact@v3`, `actions/deploy-pages@v4`.                                                                          |
| R-L3b  | Pages workflow must declare `pages: write`, `id-token: write` and the `github-pages` environment.              | Done: `pages.yml` declares both permissions and `environment: name: github-pages`.                                                                                                           |
| R-L3c  | Pages workflow must publish on every push to `main` and be re-runnable via `workflow_dispatch`.                | Done: both triggers are present.                                                                                                                                                             |
| R-L3d  | Pages workflow must declare `timeout-minutes` on every job, in line with `release.yml` policy.                 | Done: `pages.yml` sets `timeout-minutes: 10` on both `build` and `deploy`.                                                                                                                   |
| R-L4   | The published web client must behave similarly to the mobile and desktop apps.                                 | Done: the Pages bundle is byte-identical to the bundle Electron and Capacitor embed (`js/src/web/*` copied via `js/scripts/build-pages.mjs`).                                                |
| R-L4a  | The deployed bundle must include a `404.html` SPA fallback.                                                    | Done: `js/scripts/build-pages.mjs` writes `404.html` as a copy of `index.html`.                                                                                                              |
| R-L4b  | The deployed bundle must include a `.nojekyll` marker.                                                         | Done: `js/scripts/build-pages.mjs` writes `.nojekyll`.                                                                                                                                       |
| R-L4c  | The SPA must default to a usable, write-capable, fully offline experience when no server is reachable.         | Done already by `OfflineClient` + `BrowserStore` (R-F8); reaffirmed in README and USER-GUIDE.                                                                                                |
| R-L5   | The whole flow must be checkable and testable directly from the web browser.                                   | Done: `pages.yml` `deploy` job runs a smoke `curl` against the published `page_url` and `js/tests/build-pages.test.js` covers the local artifact.                                            |
| R-L5a  | The README must include the public Pages URL prominently.                                                      | Done: README "Try it now" section is the first user-facing block.                                                                                                                            |
| R-L5b  | The README must include a "Connect to your local server" recipe.                                               | Done: README "Connect the SPA to your server" section.                                                                                                                                       |
| R-L6   | The README and documentation must be reworked to be as user-friendly as possible.                              | Done: README leads with "Try it now" → "Run a local server" → "Connect"; long form lives in `docs/USER-GUIDE.md`.                                                                            |
| R-L6a  | The README must lead with the user-facing flow before the developer-facing flow.                               | Done: developer reference moved below an explicit divider.                                                                                                                                   |
| R-L7   | The README must focus on UI-based + server startup flows.                                                      | Done: first three sections of the README are URL → Rust server → JS server.                                                                                                                  |
| R-L8   | The easiest user flow is: open Pages app + start a local Rust server, with a JS server fallback.               | Done: README "Run a local server" presents Rust first, JS fallback.                                                                                                                          |
| R-L8a  | Rust server must ship `meta-sovereign-rs serve` binary on the same wire protocol.                              | Done: covered by R-G2; reaffirmed in `docs/SERVER-PARITY.md`.                                                                                                                                |
| R-L8b  | The README must document both server commands side-by-side.                                                    | Done: `cargo run --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml -p meta-sovereign-server -- serve` and `meta-sovereign serve` shown next to each other.                                                     |
| R-L9   | Verify Rust server has all features the JS server has.                                                         | Done: `docs/SERVER-PARITY.md` documents the verified route matrix; Rust covers browser-first user flows, while the six JS-only operator-hardening endpoints have explicit JS fallback paths. |
| R-L9a  | A documented feature parity matrix must compare JS and Rust servers route-by-route.                            | Done: `docs/SERVER-PARITY.md`.                                                                                                                                                               |
| R-L10  | All JavaScript code lives in a JS-only tree.                                                                   | Done: JavaScript code, tests, scripts, examples, experiments, CLI entrypoint, and Electron shell live under `js/`.                                                                           |
| R-L10a | Repository structure should be documented and matched to the templates.                                        | Done: README "Repository structure" section explains the per-language layout and references each template.                                                                                   |
| R-L11  | All Rust code lives in a Rust-only tree.                                                                       | Done: `rust/Cargo.toml`, `rust/Cargo.lock`, and `rust/crates/` contain the Rust workspace.                                                                                                   |
| R-L12  | Apply CI/CD best practices from the four AI-driven-development pipeline templates and report any gap upstream. | Done: `docs/case-studies/issue-8/ci-cd-template-comparison.md` walks every workflow and script; one upstream gap (Pages workflow back-port) is documented.                                   |
| R-L12a | Comparison must enumerate every workflow file and script.                                                      | Done: comparison covers `release.yml`, `links.yml`, `pages.yml`, all 30 scripts in `js/scripts/`.                                                                                            |
| R-L12b | Any gap that the templates themselves have must surface as a recommendation.                                   | Done: upstream proposal text included in the comparison doc, ready to paste into the JS template repo.                                                                                       |
| R-L13  | Compile a deep case study at `docs/case-studies/issue-8/`.                                                     | Done: `README.md`, `requirements.md`, `solution-plan.md`, `components.md`, `external-research.md`, `ci-cd-template-comparison.md`, `data/`.                                                  |

## M. Newbie-friendly UI (issue #10)

The following requirements arose from
[issue #10](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/issues/10) —
"We need to rework the UI, so we don't show sections with empty data".
The full atomic list and traceability live in
[`docs/case-studies/issue-10/requirements.md`](./case-studies/issue-10/requirements.md);
the canonical surface is summarised below.

| ID    | Requirement                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | State                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ----- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-M1  | No SPA section may render a bare empty-data placeholder. Every nav surface (chat, operator, contacts, automation, patterns, replies, facts, audience, broadcast, outreach, profile, backup, status) must, when empty, render a connection guide instead. | Done: `js/src/web/connection-guides.js` exports a `connectionGuides` registry covering every nav surface; `views.js` renders `<ConnectionGuide section="..." />` on empty data. |
| R-M2  | Each connection guide names the providers / data sources that fill the section and links to (or inlines) the install / connect steps for each.                                                                                                           | Done: every entry in `connectionGuides` lists its providers with inline install / connect copy.                                                                                 |
| R-M3  | Each connection guide explains how to **export the data from the external profile** and load it into the app (archive import path).                                                                                                                      | Done: each provider entry has an `archive` block with the file-name conventions and the import paragraph.                                                                       |
| R-M4  | Each connection guide explains how to provide **API credentials** for the live API connector to the same provider, in-app.                                                                                                                               | Done: each provider entry has an `apiCredentials` block with the canonical env var (e.g. `VK_ACCESS_TOKEN`) and the docs link to obtain it.                                     |
| R-M5  | The SPA must attempt to connect to a provider API directly from the browser when the user supplies credentials, and detect CORS failures.                                                                                                                | Done: `tryDirect()` in `connection-guides.js` performs a browser-side `fetch()` and reports the result.                                                                         |
| R-M6  | When a CORS failure is detected, the SPA must **not** fail silently. It must show in-app instructions on how to start a local server (which will proxy).                                                                                                 | Done: `tryDirect()` classifies the failure and the `LocalServerHelp` component is rendered inline.                                                                              |
| R-M7  | The local-server install instructions must live inside the application — the user must not need to leave the SPA (no "see GitHub README" hand-off).                                                                                                      | Done: install copy lives inside `LocalServerHelp` (npm / Rust commands inline).                                                                                                 |
| R-M8  | When offline (no local server discovered) the SPA must still try to talk to provider APIs directly. Only when this fails because of CORS does it recommend the server.                                                                                   | Done: `tryDirect()` runs even when `discoverServer()` returned no server; only the CORS-classified branch falls back to the local-server CTA.                                   |
| R-M9  | The SPA must include a **tutorial layer** that walks the user through the key flows step by step.                                                                                                                                                        | Done: `js/src/web/tutorial.js` exports `TutorialOverlay` with the headline-flow steps; mounted from `app.js`.                                                                   |
| R-M10 | Each tutorial step must be skippable individually.                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Done: every step renders a "Skip step" button that advances past the current step without aborting the rest.                                                                    |
| R-M11 | The tutorial must be turn-off-able completely (a single off switch).                                                                                                                                                                                     | Done: "Turn off tutorial" stores the preference in `localStorage` so the overlay does not reappear.                                                                             |
| R-M12 | The tutorial must be re-enable-able from the UI at any later time.                                                                                                                                                                                       | Done: a persistent "Tutorial" button in the app header lets the user re-open the overlay at any time.                                                                           |
| R-M13 | `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` must contain all requirements ever recorded — including these — listed without grouping by issue (the grouping is informational; every row appears in the same global list).                                                      | Done: this section.                                                                                                                                                             |
| R-M14 | A deep case study lives at `docs/case-studies/issue-10/` (this folder), with online research for additional facts and data.                                                                                                                              | Done: `docs/case-studies/issue-10/{README,requirements,solution-plan,components,external-research}.md` + raw issue/comment JSON in `data/`.                                     |
| R-M15 | The case study must enumerate all requirements from the issue.                                                                                                                                                                                           | Done: `docs/case-studies/issue-10/requirements.md` lists R-M1 .. R-M18.                                                                                                         |
| R-M16 | The case study must propose possible solutions and a solution plan for each requirement.                                                                                                                                                                 | Done: `docs/case-studies/issue-10/solution-plan.md`.                                                                                                                            |
| R-M17 | The case study must check known existing components / libraries that solve a similar problem.                                                                                                                                                            | Done: `docs/case-studies/issue-10/components.md` + `external-research.md`.                                                                                                      |
| R-M18 | All work must land in a single PR (#11) and iterate until every requirement is fully addressed.                                                                                                                                                          | Done: PR #11 on branch `issue-10-a884af8ade4e`.                                                                                                                                 |

## N. Tutorial progress persistence (issue #13)

> IDs in this section are cited as `R-N<k> (tutorial)`. See [Known ID clashes](#known-id-clashes).

The following requirements arose from
[issue #13](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/issues/13) -
"After page refresh progress of tutorial is not saved". The full
atomic list and traceability live in
[`docs/case-studies/issue-13/requirements.md`](./case-studies/issue-13/requirements.md).

| ID   | Requirement                                                                                                                         | State                                                                                                                                        |
| ---- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-N1 | Tutorial step progress must survive a full page refresh.                                                                            | Done: `TutorialOverlay` initializes from `readProgressIndex(storage, steps)` and resumes a stored `stepId`.                                  |
| R-N2 | The tutorial must persist progress when the user advances with `Next`.                                                              | Done: `advance()` writes the next step through `writeProgressPreference()`.                                                                  |
| R-N3 | The tutorial must persist progress when the user skips an individual step.                                                          | Done: `Skip step` uses the same persisted advance path as `Next`.                                                                            |
| R-N4 | Completing the tutorial must persist a completed/off state so refresh does not reopen it from step 1.                               | Done: the final advance writes `{ off: true, completed: true, at }` through `writeCompletedPreference()`.                                    |
| R-N5 | Reopening the tutorial from the header must still start a fresh tutorial after a stored off/completed preference is cleared.        | Done: `useTutorialPreference().reopen()` clears the storage key before opening; the overlay reads current storage instead of stale memoized. |
| R-N6 | Raw issue and PR data, comments, reviews, and screenshots must be compiled under `docs/case-studies/issue-13/`.                     | Done: data, the issue screenshot, and the after-fix screenshot are captured under `docs/case-studies/issue-13/{data,assets}/`.               |
| R-N7 | The case study must reconstruct the timeline, requirements, root cause, known components/libraries, and solution plans.             | Done: `README.md`, `requirements.md`, `solution-plan.md`, and `components.md`.                                                               |
| R-N8 | External research must be recorded for the browser and React state behavior that explains the bug.                                  | Done: `external-research.md` records MDN Web Storage and React state/effect references.                                                      |
| R-N9 | If the root cause is in another project, an upstream GitHub issue must be filed with a reproduction and workaround; otherwise note. | Done: root cause is local to this repo's tutorial storage contract; `README.md` records that no upstream issue is required.                  |

## O. Email support (issue #3)

> IDs in this section are cited as `R-N<k> (email)`. See [Known ID clashes](#known-id-clashes).

The following requirements arose from
[issue #3](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/issues/3) —
"Add email support". The full atomic list and traceability live in
[`docs/case-studies/issue-3/requirements.md`](./case-studies/issue-3/requirements.md);
the canonical surface is summarised below.

| ID    | Requirement                                                                                                       | State                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ----- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-N1  | Compile issue #3 research and evidence under `docs/case-studies/issue-3/`.                                        | Done: raw issue/comment JSON plus README, requirements, solution plan, components, research notes, and external research files are present.                                                          |
| R-N2  | Research email protocols, browser constraints, and existing provider APIs before implementation.                  | Done: `external-research.md` records JMAP, IMAP, POP3, SMTP, Gmail, Microsoft Graph, Fetch, and CORS source notes.                                                                                   |
| R-N3  | Enumerate every requirement from the issue and track completion.                                                  | Done: `docs/case-studies/issue-3/requirements.md` lists R-N1..R-N10.                                                                                                                                 |
| R-N4  | Support email imports from provider exports and unified mail archive formats.                                     | Done: `emailSource.parseArchive()` handles `.eml`, mbox, provider JSON arrays, and common message-list envelope shapes.                                                                              |
| R-N5  | Support receiving mail through live provider APIs where browser-direct access is possible.                        | Done: `createEmailLive()` pulls Gmail, Microsoft Graph, and JMAP messages over `fetch()` and normalizes them to `msg:email:*` links.                                                                 |
| R-N6  | Support sending mail through live provider APIs where browser-direct access is possible.                          | Done: Gmail `users.messages.send`, Microsoft Graph `sendMail`, and JMAP `Email/set` + `EmailSubmission/set` are implemented.                                                                         |
| R-N7  | Support all existing email protocols by using a local server fallback for protocols browsers cannot use directly. | Done: IMAP, POP3, and SMTP paths attach the Node local-server raw protocol transport and still return `LOCAL_SERVER_REQUIRED` in browser-only contexts.                                              |
| R-N8  | Add local server endpoints for email import and send fallback.                                                    | Done: the JS server exposes `POST /api/email/pull` and `POST /api/email/send`, stores stamped imported links, and reads `secret:email:*` tokens from the store.                                      |
| R-N9  | Add CLI/API surfaces so email support is available outside the SPA.                                               | Done: `source-pull --source=email ...`, `email-send ...`, `./sources/email` package export, and source registry integration expose the feature.                                                      |
| R-N10 | Verify the fix with reproducing automated tests.                                                                  | Done: source parser tests, live connector tests, raw protocol transport tests, and server-route tests cover archive import, Gmail, Graph, JMAP, IMAP/POP3/SMTP, and the local server fallback route. |

## P. Provider connections (issue #16)

The following requirements arose from
[issue #16](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/issues/16) -
"Connections to Telegram, Facebook, WhatsApp are not working as expected".
The full atomic list and traceability live in
[`docs/case-studies/issue-16/requirements.md`](./case-studies/issue-16/requirements.md).

| ID    | Requirement                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | State                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-O1  | The "Try directly" button must never fire a request that is guaranteed to 404/400 because credentials are missing.                                                                                                                   | Done: `buildProbeUrl({ provider, credentials })` returns `null` when required fields are absent; the button is disabled with an explanatory placeholder.                                                                                          |
| R-O2  | Probe URLs must interpolate the saved credential into the request URL/headers (Telegram bot token in path, Meta Graph `access_token` query, Bearer headers, etc).                                                                    | Done: each provider in `providerCatalogue` declares `probeUrlTemplate` + optional `probeHeaders`; `buildProbeUrl`/`buildProbeHeaders` interpolate `{token}`/`{phoneNumberId}`/`{appId}` from secret:\* links.                                     |
| R-O3  | The SPA must expose a typed `<input>` per credential field per provider (no shell required).                                                                                                                                         | Done: `<ProviderCredentialsForm>` in `js/src/web/settings-view.js` renders one `<input>` per `provider.apiCredentials.fields[*]` (text/password) and writes them as `secret:*` links via `api.put`.                                               |
| R-O4  | The SPA must expose a file-upload input per provider for archive imports.                                                                                                                                                            | Done: `<ProviderArchiveImport>` renders `<input type="file" accept={provider.archive.accept}>` and feeds the file bytes to the matching `parseArchive` adapter.                                                                                   |
| R-O5  | The SPA must also accept paste-in archive contents as a fallback.                                                                                                                                                                    | Done: same component renders a `<textarea>` + "Import pasted contents" button that re-uses the same parse path.                                                                                                                                   |
| R-O6  | Every provider connection must live on a single Settings nav surface.                                                                                                                                                                | Done: new `settings` view (`SettingsView` in `settings-view.js`) iterates `providerCatalogue` and renders one card per provider with anchor `id="conn-{providerId}"`.                                                                             |
| R-O7  | Per-section guides must surface a "Connect first" CTA that deep-links into the matching Settings card.                                                                                                                               | Done: `<SettingsConnectFirstCta>` in `connection-guide.js` dispatches a `meta-sovereign:navigate` event; `app.js` listens for it and switches the active view + sets `location.hash` to `#conn-{providerId}`.                                     |
| R-O8  | Saved credentials must remain encrypted at rest and never be broadcast to peers.                                                                                                                                                     | Done (pre-existing contract preserved): `wrapSecretStore` (`js/src/storage/secret-store.js`) encrypts `secret:*` payloads with AES-256-GCM; `createPeer` filters them on outbound + inbound sync.                                                 |
| R-O9  | Probe responses must be classified into ok/http/cors/network and surfaced as plain-language messages.                                                                                                                                | Done: `<SettingsProbeRow>` calls `tryDirect` and renders one of four tones (`probe-tone-ok`, `-warn`, `-error`, `-idle`); `data-probe-status` exposes the tone for tests.                                                                         |
| R-O10 | A CORS classification must surface a `<LocalServerHelp>` block with copy-pasteable Docker / npx / manual-override commands.                                                                                                          | Done: `<SettingsProbeRow>` renders `<LocalServerHelp>` from `connection-guide.js` whenever `result.classification === 'cors'`.                                                                                                                    |
| R-O11 | Unit tests must cover probe URL templates, store interactions, secret-store round-trip, and the "Connect first" CTA wiring.                                                                                                          | Done: `connection-guides-templates.test.js` (14 unit tests covering probe URL templates, header interpolation, `connectFirst` provider validation, and `credentialsFromLinks`).                                                                   |
| R-O12 | Integration test must boot the in-process server and walk the Settings → store → probe path, asserting the credential is encrypted on disk.                                                                                          | Done: `settings-credentials-roundtrip.test.js` boots `startServer({ secretPassphrase })`, persists a token via `PUT /links`, asserts ciphertext on disk and that `buildProbeUrl` interpolates the new template.                                   |
| R-O13 | End-to-end Playwright test must load the locally-built SPA, open Settings, type a fake token into the Telegram input, click "Try directly", and assert the status badge is `http`/`cors`/`network` (never the legacy 404/400 shape). | Done: `stepConnectionsCredentialRoundtrip` in `js/tests/e2e-browser-spa.mjs` types a token into `[data-field-input="token"]`, saves it, and asserts the probe button becomes enabled and `data-probe-status` reflects the live tone.              |
| R-O14 | End-to-end Playwright test must run against the deployed GitHub Pages URL after the Pages workflow finishes.                                                                                                                         | Done: `js/tests/e2e-pages.mjs` runs in the `e2e-deployed` job of `.github/workflows/pages.yml` after every deploy, against the deployed URL with a local server started in the job; pull requests run it against the built bundle (`e2e-bundle`). |
| R-O15 | A deep case study lives at `docs/case-studies/issue-16/`, with online research for additional facts and data.                                                                                                                        | Done: `docs/case-studies/issue-16/` contains `data/`, screenshots, `README.md`, `requirements.md`, `solution-plan.md`, `components.md`, `external-research.md`.                                                                                   |
| R-O16 | The case study must enumerate all requirements from the issue.                                                                                                                                                                       | Done: `requirements.md` lists R-O1..R-O19.                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| R-O17 | The case study must propose possible solutions and a solution plan for each requirement.                                                                                                                                             | Done: `solution-plan.md`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| R-O18 | The case study must check known existing components / libraries that solve a similar problem.                                                                                                                                        | Done: `components.md` + `external-research.md`.                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| R-O19 | All work lands in a single PR (#17) and iterates until every requirement is fully addressed.                                                                                                                                         | Done: PR #17 merged on 2026-05-04.                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
