## Q. UI and documentation internationalisation (issue #18)

The following requirements arose from
[issue #18](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/issues/18) -
"Support i18n of UI for en (english), ch (chinese), hi (hindi), and
ru (russian)" and the follow-up PR #19 review comment asking for
Markdown documentation parity. The full atomic list and traceability
live in
[`docs/case-studies/issue-18/requirements.md`](./case-studies/issue-18/requirements.md).

| ID    | Requirement                                                                                                                                        | State                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ----- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-Q1  | The SPA must let users switch the visible UI language between English, Chinese, Hindi, and Russian.                                                | Done: the header language switcher uses `availableLocales` and `useLocale()` from `js/src/web/i18n.js`.                                                                                   |
| R-Q2  | The first visit must auto-detect the closest supported language from browser data, then allow an explicit app override.                            | Done: `detectInitialLocale()` reads `localStorage.metaSovereignLocale`, then `navigator.languages`, then `navigator.language`, and falls back to `en`.                                    |
| R-Q3  | Locale selection must persist locally without leaking to peers.                                                                                    | Done: explicit locale picks are stored only in `localStorage`; peer sync continues to filter preferences and secrets.                                                                     |
| R-Q4  | Changing language must update accessibility metadata.                                                                                              | Done: `applyLocale()` writes `<html lang>` and `<html dir>` on every locale change.                                                                                                       |
| R-Q5  | Every authored, user-facing SPA string must move behind a stable translation key.                                                                  | Done: nav, shell, tutorial, settings, guides, view headings, placeholders, status labels, and common actions use `t()`; provider names and API identifiers remain source data.            |
| R-Q6  | All four locale dictionaries must stay structurally identical.                                                                                     | Done: `js/tests/i18n.test.js` asserts non-English dictionaries expose the same flattened key set as `en`.                                                                                 |
| R-Q7  | User-facing Markdown docs must expose language-switcher headers and translated siblings for `en`, `zh`, `hi`, and `ru`.                            | Done: `README`, `CHANGELOG`, `mobile/README`, top-level `docs/*.md`, and issue-18 case-study docs have `*.zh.md`, `*.hi.md`, and `*.ru.md` siblings with H1 switchers matching hive-mind. |
| R-Q8  | Markdown localisation must be covered by automation so new docs cannot silently drop a language sibling or switcher link.                          | Done: `js/tests/docs-language.test.js` checks the tracked user-facing Markdown groups, validates sibling files, and verifies each switcher link resolves.                                 |
| R-Q9  | A deep case study must capture issue data, online research, requirements, component survey, and solution plan under `docs/case-studies/issue-18/`. | Done: the issue-18 folder contains raw issue data plus `README`, `requirements`, `solution-plan`, `components`, and `external-research` in all four requested languages.                  |
| R-Q10 | All work lands in single PR #19 and iterates until every requirement is addressed.                                                                 | Done: PR #19 merged on 2026-05-04.                                                                                                                                                        |

---

## R. GitHub support (issue #5)

The following requirements arose from
[issue #5](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/issues/5) -
"Add GitHub support". The full atomic list, evidence, component
survey, and traceability live in
[`docs/case-studies/issue-5/`](./case-studies/issue-5/README.md).

| ID    | Requirement                                                                                                                                           | State                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ----- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-R1  | Treat GitHub as a first-class `MessageSource` so issues, issue comments, pull requests, PR review comments, and discussions become normalized links.  | Done: `js/src/sources/github.js` registers a `github` source with `parseArchive`/`live.pullMessages` covering every comment-bearing surface.                                      |
| R-R2  | Allow archive imports of GitHub data (raw JSON exports from the REST API or `gh api` dumps).                                                          | Done: `parseArchive` accepts both an array of issues/PRs/comments and the standard `{issues:[], comments:[], pulls:[], reviews:[], reviewComments:[], discussions:[]}` envelope.  |
| R-R3  | Pull live data through the GitHub REST API.                                                                                                           | Done: `createGithubLive().pullMessages` walks repo issues, PRs, both comment streams, and review summaries with `Bearer` auth and a configurable `baseUrl`.                       |
| R-R4  | Support browser-direct calls when CORS allows; fall back to the local server for raw clones and authenticated bulk pulls.                             | Done: live calls use `requestJson()` so the SPA can run them in-browser; the JS server adds `POST /api/github/pull` and `POST /api/github/clone` for same-origin proxying.        |
| R-R5  | Allow posting comments back into GitHub (issues and pull requests) so the unified inbox stays bidirectional.                                          | Done: `createGithubLive().post(content, { issueNumber })` issues `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/issues/{n}/comments` and is wired through CLI and server entrypoints.                |
| R-R6  | Allow downloading every repository the user owns and index the working tree as links in the local store.                                              | Done: `cloneRepo()` fetches the GitHub `tarball` archive, walks the entries, and writes one `repo:<owner>/<name>:file:<path>` link per file plus a `repo:<owner>/<name>` index.   |
| R-R7  | Index repository metadata (description, default branch, topics, last push) alongside the file tree so the CRM can filter by repo.                     | Done: each clone seeds a `repo:<owner>/<name>` link with metadata children.                                                                                                       |
| R-R8  | Expose `listRepos()` so the SPA, CLI, and server can enumerate the user's repositories before deciding what to clone.                                 | Done: `createGithubLive().listRepos()` paginates `/user/repos` and returns plain JSON the SPA can render.                                                                         |
| R-R9  | Add CLI subcommands so contributors can drive every feature without booting the SPA.                                                                  | Done: `source-pull --source=github`, `github-clone --owner=<o> --repo=<r>`, and `github-comment --issue=<n> --text=<t>` cover pull, clone, and post.                              |
| R-R10 | Add same-origin server routes so the browser SPA can use the JS server as a proxy when GitHub blocks cross-origin requests.                           | Done: `POST /api/github/pull`, `POST /api/github/clone`, and `POST /api/github/post-comment` route to `createGithubLive()` and stamp imported links via `stampSourceLink`.        |
| R-R11 | Add a connection guide entry for GitHub in the SPA Settings catalogue so users can paste a personal access token and probe `/user`.                   | Done: `providerCatalogue.github` declares the archive accept filter, `secret:github:token` field, `https://api.github.com/user` probe URL, and `Bearer` headers.                  |
| R-R12 | Honour the existing secret-store contract (`secret:*` links, AES-256-GCM at rest) for GitHub PATs.                                                    | Done: the connection guide writes the token into `secret:github:access-token`, which `wrapSecretStore` already encrypts.                                                          |
| R-R13 | Compile issue #5 research and evidence under `docs/case-studies/issue-5/`.                                                                            | Done: the folder contains `data/issue.json`, `data/comments.json`, `README.md`, `requirements.md`, `solution-plan.md`, `components.md`, and `external-research.md`.               |
| R-R14 | Research existing GitHub integration libraries (Octokit, `gh`, `isomorphic-git`, `simple-git`) and the GitHub REST API surface before implementation. | Done: `external-research.md` and `components.md` capture the libraries, why a small dependency-free adapter was preferred, and the relevant REST endpoints.                       |
| R-R15 | Enumerate every requirement from the issue and propose a solution per requirement.                                                                    | Done: `requirements.md` lists `R-R1..R-R18` and `solution-plan.md` maps them to deliverables.                                                                                     |
| R-R16 | Ship reproducing automated tests before the implementation.                                                                                           | Done: `js/tests/github-source.test.js` covers archive import, live `pullMessages`, `listRepos`, `cloneRepo` indexing, and `post()` comment creation with an injected `fetchImpl`. |
| R-R17 | Update top-level `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` and `README.md` so future contributors discover GitHub support.                                               | Done: this section adds the `R-R*` table; `README.md` lists GitHub alongside the other ten networks.                                                                              |
| R-R18 | Land everything in a single pull request that iterates until each requirement is fully addressed.                                                     | Done: PR #22 merged on 2026-05-06.                                                                                                                                                |

## S. Upwork support (issue #4)

The following requirements arose from
[issue #4](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/issues/4) -
"Add upwork support". The full atomic list, evidence, component
survey, and traceability live in
[`docs/case-studies/issue-4/`](./case-studies/issue-4/README.md).

| ID    | Requirement                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | State                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ----- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-S1  | Treat Upwork as a first-class `MessageSource` so messages, proposals, contracts, jobs, and rooms become normalized links in the links data store.                                                                                         | Done: `js/src/sources/upwork.js` registers an `upwork` source with `parseArchive`/`live.pullMessages` covering every comment-bearing surface and additional `job:`, `proposal:`, `contract:`, `room:`, `transaction:` link prefixes.                                                                            |
| R-S2  | Allow archive imports of Upwork data (GraphQL JSON dumps and CSV exports such as the Reports → Transaction History download).                                                                                                             | Done: `parseArchive(input)` accepts (a) an array of GraphQL nodes / payloads, (b) the structured envelope `{ jobs, proposals, contracts, rooms, messages, transactions, timeLogs }`, and (c) raw CSV strings sniffed against the Upwork transaction-history header.                                             |
| R-S3  | Pull live data through the Upwork GraphQL API at `https://api.upwork.com/graphql` using OAuth 2.0 Bearer tokens.                                                                                                                          | Done: `createUpworkLive({ token, fetchImpl, baseUrl, organizationId })` posts GraphQL operations through `requestJson()` with `Authorization: Bearer <PAT>` and follows Relay-style cursor pagination via `pageInfo.endCursor`.                                                                                 |
| R-S4  | Support job/task search from the freelancer perspective so users can see open marketplace jobs.                                                                                                                                           | Done: `createUpworkLive().searchJobs({ query, sort, limit })` runs `marketplaceJobPostingsSearch` and yields `job:upwork:<id>` links plus rows the SPA can render.                                                                                                                                              |
| R-S5  | Cover communication **before** worker approval (proposals to a job, proposal-room messages from both sides).                                                                                                                              | Done: `createUpworkLive().pullMessages({ stage: 'proposal', jobId })` walks proposal listings + their proposal-room messages, normalised through `buildMessageLink()` with a `proposal:` chat namespace.                                                                                                        |
| R-S6  | Cover communication **after** worker approval (contract rooms, room messages).                                                                                                                                                            | Done: `createUpworkLive().pullMessages({ stage: 'contract', contractId })` (and the default `stage:'all'`) walks contract rooms and their messages with a `room:` chat namespace.                                                                                                                               |
| R-S7  | Both the **client (task giver)** and **freelancer (task taker)** perspectives are supported — same adapter, perspective is a parameter rather than a separate code path.                                                                  | Done: `pullMessages({ perspective: 'client' \| 'freelancer' \| 'both' })` selects which jobs / contracts / rooms to enumerate; `parseArchive` keeps both sides side by side.                                                                                                                                    |
| R-S8  | Allow posting messages back into Upwork rooms so the unified inbox stays bidirectional.                                                                                                                                                   | Done: `createUpworkLive().post({ text }, { roomId })` issues the `roomsCreateMessage` GraphQL mutation; the mutation name is configurable via `mutationName` because Upwork's documented field is unverified publicly.                                                                                          |
| R-S9  | Allow ingesting account-level Upwork data exports (the GDPR / DSAR bundle, the Reports → Transaction History CSV, the Weekly Summary CSV).                                                                                                | Done: `parseArchive` recognises the GDPR JSON envelope (`messages`, `rooms`, `contracts`, `jobs`, `proposals`, `transactions`, `time_logs`) and CSV strings (header sniffed against `Date,Type,Description,Amount,Balance,Freelancer,Team,Account name,PO,Ref ID`).                                             |
| R-S10 | Index Upwork **jobs** as their own link kind so the CRM and audience filters can group threads by job/task.                                                                                                                               | Done: `job:upwork:<id>` links are written for every job seen, with `children` pointing at related proposal/contract/room links.                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| R-S11 | Index Upwork **contracts** as their own link kind so reporting / hours / earnings can be related back without re-walking message bodies.                                                                                                  | Done: `contract:upwork:<id>` links carry `clientId`, `freelancerId`, `jobId`, `startDate`, `status`, and `children` linking to the room and the time logs.                                                                                                                                                      |
| R-S12 | Honour Upwork's API ToS — no permanent caching of Upwork-derived content past 24 hours without user consent; user-imported export rows are durable.                                                                                       | Done: live-pulled links carry `cacheTtlMs: 86_400_000` and a `softCache: true` flag; archive-imported links are written without a TTL. The TTL is enforced by `softCacheRetention()` exported from the adapter.                                                                                                 |
| R-S13 | Add CLI subcommands so contributors can drive every feature without booting the SPA.                                                                                                                                                      | Done: `source-pull --source=upwork [--stage=…] [--perspective=…] [--organization-id=…]`, `upwork-search --query=<q>`, `upwork-message --room-id=<r> --text=<t>`, plus `source-import --source=upwork --file=<path>` for bundles.                                                                                |
| R-S14 | Add same-origin server routes so the browser SPA can use the JS server as a proxy when Upwork blocks cross-origin requests.                                                                                                               | Done: `POST /api/upwork/pull`, `POST /api/upwork/search`, and `POST /api/upwork/post-message` route to `createUpworkLive()`, read the OAuth token from `secret:upwork:access-token` (or `secret:upwork:token` as a fallback), and stamp imported links via `stampSourceLink`.                                   |
| R-S15 | Add a connection guide entry for Upwork in the SPA Settings catalogue so users can paste an OAuth access token (and optionally a refresh token / org id) and probe the GraphQL endpoint.                                                  | Done: `providerCatalogue.upwork` declares the archive accept filter (`.json,.csv`), `secret:upwork:access-token` password field, optional `secret:upwork:refresh-token` and `secret:upwork:organization-id`, and a `https://api.upwork.com/graphql` probe POST that runs the `query { user { id } }` operation. |
| R-S16 | Honour the existing secret-store contract (`secret:*` links, AES-256-GCM at rest) for Upwork OAuth tokens.                                                                                                                                | Done: the connection guide writes the access token into `secret:upwork:access-token` and the refresh token into `secret:upwork:refresh-token`; both are encrypted automatically by `wrapSecretStore`.                                                                                                           |
| R-S17 | Compile issue #4 research and evidence under `docs/case-studies/issue-4/`.                                                                                                                                                                | Done: the folder contains `data/issue.json`, `data/comments.json`, `README.md`, `requirements.md`, `solution-plan.md`, `components.md`, and `external-research.md`.                                                                                                                                             |
| R-S18 | Research existing Upwork integration libraries (`@upwork/node-upwork-oauth2`, `python-upwork-oauth2`, third-party MCP wrappers) and the Upwork GraphQL API surface before implementation.                                                 | Done: `external-research.md` and `components.md` capture the libraries, why a small dependency-free adapter was preferred, the relevant GraphQL operations, OAuth flow, rate limits, and the ToS constraints.                                                                                                   |
| R-S19 | Enumerate every requirement from the issue and propose a solution per requirement.                                                                                                                                                        | Done: `requirements.md` lists `R-S1..R-S22` and `solution-plan.md` maps them to deliverables.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| R-S20 | Ship reproducing automated tests before the implementation (archive import for envelope + array + CSV, live `searchJobs` / `pullMessages` / `post` with an injected `fetchImpl`, source-registry membership, `softCacheRetention` purge). | Done: `js/tests/upwork-source.test.js` covers archive import (envelope + array + CSV), `searchJobs`, `pullMessages` for both perspectives, `post`, source-registry integration, and the soft-cache retention helper.                                                                                            |
| R-S21 | Update top-level `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` and `README.md` so future contributors discover Upwork support.                                                                                                                                   | Done: this section adds the `R-S*` table; `README.md` lists Upwork alongside the existing networks.                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| R-S22 | Land everything in a single pull request that iterates until each requirement is fully addressed.                                                                                                                                         | Done: PR #23 merged on 2026-05-06.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

---

## T. PeoplePerHour support (issue #12)

The following requirements arose from
[issue #12](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/issues/12) -
"Add people per hour support". The full atomic list, evidence, component
survey, and traceability live in
[`docs/case-studies/issue-12/`](./case-studies/issue-12/README.md).

The issue body's reference to "exports from upwork" is a copy/paste
artefact from the immediately preceding Upwork issue (#4) — the title
is treated as authoritative; this section ships a PeoplePerHour
adapter.

| ID    | Requirement                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | State                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ----- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| R-T1  | Treat PeoplePerHour as a first-class `MessageSource` so messages, proposals, workstreams, projects, and rooms become normalized links in the links data store.                                                                                | Done: `js/src/sources/peopleperhour.js` registers a `peopleperhour` source with `parseArchive`/`live.pullMessages` covering every comment-bearing surface and `project:`, `proposal:`, `workstream:`, `room:`, `invoice:`, `hourstream:` link prefixes.                                                            |
| R-T2  | Allow archive imports of PeoplePerHour data (JSON dumps and CSV exports such as the Earnings report).                                                                                                                                         | Done: `parseArchive(input)` accepts (a) an array of REST nodes / payloads, (b) the structured envelope `{ projects, proposals, workstreams, rooms, messages, invoices, hourstreams }`, and (c) raw CSV strings sniffed against the PeoplePerHour Earnings header.                                                  |
| R-T3  | Pull live data through the PeoplePerHour API at `https://www.peopleperhour.com/api/v1` using OAuth 2.0 Bearer tokens.                                                                                                                         | Done: `createPeoplePerHourLive({ token, fetchImpl, baseUrl })` calls REST endpoints through `requestJson()` with `Authorization: Bearer <PAT>` and follows cursor pagination via the `nextCursor` field returned by the API.                                                                                       |
| R-T4  | Support project/task search from the freelancer perspective so users can see open marketplace projects.                                                                                                                                       | Done: `createPeoplePerHourLive().searchProjects({ query, sort, limit })` runs `GET /projects/search` and yields `project:peopleperhour:<id>` links plus rows the SPA can render.                                                                                                                                   |
| R-T5  | Cover communication **before** worker approval (proposals to a project, proposal-room messages from both sides).                                                                                                                              | Done: `createPeoplePerHourLive().pullMessages({ stage: 'proposal', projectId })` walks proposal listings + their proposal-room messages, normalised through `buildMessageLink()` with a `proposal:` chat namespace.                                                                                                |
| R-T6  | Cover communication **after** worker approval (workstreams, workstream messages).                                                                                                                                                             | Done: `createPeoplePerHourLive().pullMessages({ stage: 'workstream', workstreamId })` (and the default `stage: 'all'`) walks workstreams and their messages with a `room:` chat namespace.                                                                                                                         |
| R-T7  | Both the **buyer (task giver)** and **freelancer (task taker)** perspectives are supported — same adapter, perspective is a parameter rather than a separate code path.                                                                       | Done: `pullMessages({ perspective: 'buyer' \| 'freelancer' \| 'both' })` selects which projects / workstreams / rooms to enumerate; `parseArchive` keeps both sides side by side.                                                                                                                                  |
| R-T8  | Allow posting messages back into PeoplePerHour rooms so the unified inbox stays bidirectional once write access is granted.                                                                                                                   | Done: `createPeoplePerHourLive().post({ text }, { workstreamId, proposalId })` issues `POST /workstreams/{id}/messages` (or `POST /proposals/{id}/messages`). Endpoint paths are configurable via `endpointOverrides` because PeoplePerHour's developer portal is gated and several paths are unverified publicly. |
| R-T9  | Allow ingesting account-level PeoplePerHour data exports (the GDPR / DSAR bundle, the Earnings CSV).                                                                                                                                          | Done: `parseArchive` recognises the GDPR JSON envelope and CSV strings (header sniffed against `Date,Type,Description,Net,Gross,Buyer,Freelancer,Reference`). Output is one link per row, all under `msg:peopleperhour:*` and the relevant sibling kinds.                                                          |
| R-T10 | Index PeoplePerHour **projects** as their own link kind so the CRM and audience filters can group threads by project/task.                                                                                                                    | Done: `project:peopleperhour:<id>` links are written for every project seen, with `children` pointing at related proposal/workstream/room links.                                                                                                                                                                   |
| R-T11 | Index PeoplePerHour **workstreams** as their own link kind so reporting / hours / earnings can be related back without re-walking message bodies.                                                                                             | Done: `workstream:peopleperhour:<id>` links carry `buyerId`, `freelancerId`, `projectId`, `startDate`, `status`, and `children` linking to the room and the hourstream entries.                                                                                                                                    |
| R-T12 | Honour PeoplePerHour's API ToS — no permanent caching of platform-derived content past **24 hours** without user consent; user-imported export rows are durable.                                                                              | Done: live-pulled links carry `cacheTtlMs: 86_400_000` and a `softCache: true` flag; archive-imported links are written without a TTL. The TTL is enforced by `softCacheRetention()` exported from the adapter.                                                                                                    |
| R-T13 | Add CLI subcommands so contributors can drive every feature without booting the SPA.                                                                                                                                                          | Done: `source-pull --source=peopleperhour [--stage=…] [--perspective=…]`, `peopleperhour-search --query=<q>`, `peopleperhour-message --workstream-id=<w> --text=<t>`, plus `source-import --source=peopleperhour --file=<path>` for bundles.                                                                       |
| R-T14 | Add same-origin server routes so the browser SPA can use the JS server as a proxy when PeoplePerHour blocks cross-origin requests.                                                                                                            | Done: `POST /api/peopleperhour/pull`, `POST /api/peopleperhour/search`, `POST /api/peopleperhour/post-message` route to `createPeoplePerHourLive()`, read the OAuth token from `secret:peopleperhour:access-token` (or `secret:peopleperhour:token`), and stamp imported links via `stampSourceLink`.              |
| R-T15 | Add a connection guide entry for PeoplePerHour in the SPA Settings catalogue so users can paste an OAuth access token (and optionally a refresh token) and probe a known endpoint.                                                            | Done: `providerCatalogue.peopleperhour` declares the archive accept filter (`.json,.csv`), `secret:peopleperhour:access-token` password field, optional `secret:peopleperhour:refresh-token`, and a `https://www.peopleperhour.com/api/v1/me` probe GET.                                                           |
| R-T16 | Honour the existing secret-store contract (`secret:*` links, AES-256-GCM at rest) for PeoplePerHour OAuth tokens.                                                                                                                             | Done: the connection guide writes the access token into `secret:peopleperhour:access-token` and the refresh token into `secret:peopleperhour:refresh-token`; both are encrypted automatically by `wrapSecretStore`.                                                                                                |
| R-T17 | Compile issue #12 research and evidence under `docs/case-studies/issue-12/`.                                                                                                                                                                  | Done: the folder contains `data/issue.json`, `data/comments.json`, `README.md`, `requirements.md`, `solution-plan.md`, `components.md`, and `external-research.md`.                                                                                                                                                |
| R-T18 | Research existing PeoplePerHour integration libraries and the public API surface before implementation.                                                                                                                                       | Done: `external-research.md` and `components.md` capture the third-party libraries, why a small dependency-free adapter was preferred, the relevant REST endpoints, OAuth flow, rate limits, and the ToS constraints.                                                                                              |
| R-T19 | Enumerate every requirement from the issue and propose a solution per requirement.                                                                                                                                                            | Done: `requirements.md` lists `R-T1..R-T22` and `solution-plan.md` maps them to deliverables.                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| R-T20 | Ship reproducing automated tests before the implementation (archive import for envelope + array + CSV, live `searchProjects` / `pullMessages` / `post` with an injected `fetchImpl`, source-registry membership, `softCacheRetention` purge). | Done: `js/tests/peopleperhour-source.test.js` covers archive import, `searchProjects`, `pullMessages` for both perspectives, `post`, source-registry integration, and the soft-cache retention helper.                                                                                                             |
| R-T21 | Update top-level `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` and `README.md` so future contributors discover PeoplePerHour support.                                                                                                                                | Done: this section adds the `R-T*` table; `README.md` lists PeoplePerHour alongside the existing networks.                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| R-T22 | Land everything in a single pull request that iterates until each requirement is fully addressed.                                                                                                                                             | Done: PR #24 merged on 2026-05-06.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

## U. Mobile-first UI overhaul (issue #25)

> IDs in this section are cited as `R-N<k> (mobile UI)`. See [Known ID clashes](#known-id-clashes).

The following requirements arose from
[issue #25](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/issues/25) -
"the UI now must be improved". The full atomic list, evidence,
component survey, and traceability live in
[`docs/case-studies/issue-25/`](./case-studies/issue-25/README.md). The
issue reuses the `R-N*` prefix; rows are unique within the issue-25
table and cross-reference the case study via stable IDs.

| ID    | Requirement                                                                                                                       | State                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ----- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-N1  | The SPA must be mobile first - default rendering targets a phone-sized viewport.                                                  | Done: `js/src/web/shell/` exposes `<AppShell>` with a compact bottom-nav (≤640 px), a navigation rail (641-1023 px), and a permanent drawer (≥1024 px). Wired in `js/src/web/app.js`.                                                                                                                                  |
| R-N2  | UI/UX should be in the best possible way (Apple/Google/Microsoft guidance, accessible tap targets, focus rings).                  | Done: shell tap targets are 44 × 44 px or larger, `:focus-visible` rules cover every interactive control, and `safe-area-inset-bottom` is honoured. Audit cross-references live in `docs/UI-DESIGN-AUDIT.md`.                                                                                                          |
| R-N3  | All user-facing UI must be fully translated, not partially - no English literal can render when the active locale is non-English. | Done: `js/src/web/connection-guides.js` and provider labels route through `t()`; `js/tests/i18n.test.js` enforces parity across `en/ru/zh/hi`. The Russian-build "Your unified inbox starts empty." regression is fixed by `feat(i18n)` commit `98828fb`.                                                              |
| R-N4  | Each connected service should have its own item in the connections list with name, description, and connection state.             | Done: `js/src/web/connections/connections-list.js` renders one card per provider with translated name, description, and state badge. Test: `js/tests/connections-screens.test.js`.                                                                                                                                     |
| R-N5  | External-service connections should live on a separate page, like settings.                                                       | Done: a dedicated `connections` view exists in `js/src/web/nav-items.js` and `js/src/web/views.js`; Settings retains only app-level preferences.                                                                                                                                                                       |
| R-N6  | The design should clearly show whether a service is connected or not.                                                             | Done: each card declares one of `connected` / `not-connected` / `action-required` state with a translated label and a colour token (green / neutral / amber). Covered by `js/tests/connections-screens.test.js`.                                                                                                       |
| R-N7  | Tapping a "not connected" service routes the user to a separate setup screen.                                                     | Done: `<ConnectionsList>` calls an `onOpen(providerId)` handler that mounts `<ConnectionDetail>` in `js/src/web/views.js`; the list itself never inlines the credential form.                                                                                                                                          |
| R-N8  | Setup flows must explain exactly how to obtain the required data, so no separate instructions are needed.                         | Done: every provider in `js/src/web/connection-guides.js` declares `setupSteps[]` with translated `titleKey`/`bodyKey` (and optional `actionKey`/`href`). Test: `js/tests/connection-guides-templates.test.js`.                                                                                                        |
| R-N9  | The tutorial overlay must dim everything except the next click target.                                                            | Done: `js/src/web/tutorial.js` exports `<TutorialSpotlight>`; `app.css` uses a `box-shadow: 0 0 0 9999px rgba(0,0,0,0.55)` outset to dim outside the rect, with a `prefers-reduced-transparency` fallback. Tests: `js/tests/tutorial-spotlight.test.js`.                                                               |
| R-N10 | The tutorial must guide the user first to service connections, then walk through the chosen provider's setup steps.               | Done: `defaultSteps[0]` in `js/src/web/tutorial.js` is the `connect` step pointing at `nav:connections` with `tutorial.connect.{title,body}` keys translated for `en/ru/zh/hi`. Follow-up steps render `setupSteps[]` inside `<ConnectionDetail>`.                                                                     |
| R-N11 | The visual style should resemble Apple Liquid Glass (semi-transparent surfaces).                                                  | Done: `js/src/web/app.css` defines `--surface-glass` (backdrop-filter blur 28px, saturate 180%) and `.glass`/`.glass-strong` utilities; `@media (prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce)` substitutes a solid surface. Applied to top-bar, bottom-nav, drawer, modal cards, and the tutorial card.                       |
| R-N12 | Use Apple/Google/Microsoft design guidelines and Chakra UI + React.js patterns.                                                   | Done as design _guidance_ rather than a runtime swap: the redesign adopts Chakra UI's responsive tokens, semantic spacing, and accessible primitives without taking a hard dependency. The trade-off is recorded in `docs/case-studies/issue-25/components.md`; bundle-size delta is captured in PR #26's description. |

## V. CV synchronisation across job platforms (issue #29)

The following requirements arise from
[issue #29](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/issues/29) —
"make sure it is fully possible to update CV at all popular
platforms". The design, the step vocabulary, the telemetry contract
and the platform coverage table live in
[`docs/CV-SYNC.md`](./CV-SYNC.md); the research, the plan and the
verification logs live in
[`docs/case-studies/issue-29/`](./case-studies/issue-29/README.md).

| ID    | Requirement                                                                                | State                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ----- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-V1  | One canonical CV model, readable and writable as links notation.                           | Done: `js/src/cv/model.js` defines map/list/record sections, path addressing and `cvToLink`/`cvFromLink`. Tests: `js/tests/cv-model.test.js`.                                                                                                                                                             |
| R-V2  | Compare CVs across platforms and derive what each one must change.                         | Done: `js/src/cv/diff.js` keyed diff, `compareCvs` matrix and `reconcileCvs` plan. Tests: `js/tests/cv-diff.test.js`.                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| R-V3  | Plans are declarative data, inspectable without a browser.                                 | Done: `js/src/cv/platforms/steps.js` (12 actions, 3 confidence levels) and `define.js` validate every plan at registry load. Tests: `js/tests/cv-plans.test.js`.                                                                                                                                          |
| R-V4  | Server-rendered JSON blobs are preferred over CSS selectors where a site ships one.        | Done: `js/src/cv/platforms/parse.js` mappers back `extractJson` for Habr Career and VietnamWorks.                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| R-V5  | Full read/update plan for **career.habr.com**.                                             | Done: `habr-career.js` — 17 steps, 12 verified against live public markup, 2 write groups.                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| R-V6  | Full read/update plan for **hh.ru**.                                                       | Done: `hh.js` — 30 steps, 6 verified plus 2 documented from the public API, 3 write groups.                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| R-V7  | Full read/update plan for **LinkedIn**.                                                    | Done: `linkedin.js` — 32 steps, 3 write groups (`intro`, `about`, `skills`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| R-V8  | Full read/update plan for **Naukri**.                                                      | Done: `naukri.js` — 34 steps, 3 write groups (`headline`, `key-skills`, `employment`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| R-V9  | Full read/update plan for **VietnamWorks**.                                                | Done: `vietnamworks.js` — 32 steps, 3 write groups (`personal`, `preferences`, `cv-builder`).                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| R-V10 | Full read/update plan for **TopCV**.                                                       | Done: `topcv.js` — 32 steps, 3 write groups (`objective`, `expectation`, `cv-document`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| R-V11 | Full read/update plan for **superjob.ru**.                                                 | Done: `superjob.js` — 18 steps, 1 write group (`headline`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| R-V12 | Every plan is validated in CI, and unverified steps are labelled rather than hidden.       | Done: `platforms/index.js` validates each plan and counts `verified`/`documented`/`draft`; `cv-platforms`, `/api/cv/platforms` and the SPA all print the draft count.                                                                                                                                     |
| R-V13 | Telemetry makes a failed live run debuggable after the fact, with redaction on by default. | Done: `js/src/cv/telemetry.js` emits 13 event types, fingerprints markup, writes HTML/PNG artifacts under `<artifactDir>/<runId>/`, and redacts e-mails, phone numbers and long tokens. Tests: `js/tests/cv-telemetry.test.js`.                                                                           |
| R-V14 | A plan runner drives browser-commander, and a browser session persists the user's login.   | Done: `runner.js` (selector fallbacks, strict-locator narrowing, timeouts as drift) and `browser.js` (per-platform profile directory, headed/headless, optional dependency). Tests: `js/tests/cv-runner.test.js`.                                                                                         |
| R-V15 | One facade for read one / read all / compare / update / sync, usable without a browser.    | Done: `js/src/cv/index.js`; every entry point accepts an injected commander, which is how the whole flow is tested. Tests: `js/tests/cv-sync.test.js`.                                                                                                                                                    |
| R-V16 | The same operations over HTTP.                                                             | Done: `js/src/server/routes-cv.js` — `/api/cv/platforms`, `/plan`, `/stored`, `/read`, `/compare`, `/sync`, `/telemetry`. Tests: `js/tests/cv-server.test.js`.                                                                                                                                            |
| R-V17 | The same operations in the terminal.                                                       | Done: `cv-platforms`, `cv-plan`, `cv-read`, `cv-diff`, `cv-sync` (with `--paths`/`--groups`/`--apply`) and `cv-telemetry`. Tests: `js/tests/cv-cli.test.js`.                                                                                                                                              |
| R-V18 | A CV screen in the SPA showing coverage, differences and run history.                      | Done: `js/src/web/cv-view.js` plus Connections entries for the browser-only platforms; the screen states plainly that a live run needs the CLI, because a browser tab cannot drive another browser. Tests: `js/tests/cv-web.test.js`.                                                                     |
| R-V19 | The Node-only CV runtime must never enter the browser bundle.                              | Done: `js/scripts/build-web.mjs` keeps `playwright`/`browser-commander` out; enforced by `js/tests/web-bundle.test.js`.                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| R-V20 | Writes can be narrowed to specific CV paths or editor groups.                              | Done: `syncCvAcross({ paths, groups })`, `cv-sync --paths=/--groups=`, and `paths`/`groups` on `POST /api/cv/sync`; a supported-but-unselected field is reported as `deselected`, never as `unsupported`.                                                                                                 |
| R-V21 | Every plan is exercised against a real browser before it meets a real profile.             | Done: `js/tests/helpers/markup-fixture.js` rebuilds each plan's expected DOM from the plan itself and `RUN_BROWSER_E2E=1 npm run test:e2e:cv` serves it at the platforms' own URLs through Playwright interception, driving all seven plans.                                                              |
| R-V22 | A backend without the CV routes degrades visibly, never silently.                          | Done: `serverFetch` in `js/src/web/dom.js` resolves against the discovered origin and treats a 404 (the Rust server's catch-all) as "unavailable", so the screen shows the "start the JS server" notice instead of an empty table. Tests: `js/tests/web-server-fetch.test.js`, `js/tests/cv-web.test.js`. |

## W. VK token sources after the Kate Mobile ban (issue #32)

The following requirements arise from
[issue #32](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/issues/32) —
"research which applications still have elevated permissions like kate
mobile previously had … reduce probability of using untrusted and small
applications". The research, the probe results and every source live in
[`docs/case-studies/issue-32/`](./case-studies/issue-32/README.md).

| ID   | Requirement                                                                                       | State                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ---- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-W1 | Every client listed on vkhost.github.io is checked against VK's current OAuth gate.               | Done: `experiments/issue-32/probe-vk-oauth-apps.mjs` probes all 15 vkhost clients, 6 official VK clients and 2 controls on oauth.vk.com and oauth.vk.ru without an account; logs in the case study. |
| R-W2 | The findings are reusable as data, ranked so first-party VK clients come before small publishers. | Done: `VK_TOKEN_SOURCES` and `rankVkTokenSources()` in `js/src/sources/vk-auth.js`; third-party clients are excluded unless asked for. Tests: `js/tests/vk-auth.test.js`.                           |
| R-W3 | A user can check what a token they already hold can still do, using documented methods only.      | Done: `inspectVkToken()` (`users.get`, `account.getAppPermissions`, `messages.getConversations`) and `describeVkError()`; CLI demo `examples/vk-token-check.mjs`.                                   |
| R-W4 | The in-app VK guide must not send users down a path VK has closed.                                | Done: `connections.vk.api.hint` in all four locales now points to the archive, community tokens and, at the user's risk, VK's own web client; it names the right secret id.                         |
| R-W5 | The VK connector follows VK's move to the vk.ru domain.                                           | Done: `createVkLive` and the connection guide default to `https://api.vk.ru`; docs link to dev.vk.ru.                                                                                               |

## X. Automations, audiences and the chat (issue #31, PR #34)

The following requirements arise from
[issue #31](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/issues/31) —
"support messaging to all personal contacts we were not talking for
long" — and from the
[PR #34 review](https://github.com/link-foundation/meta-sovereign/pull/34),
which asked for a universal, configurable automation instead of a
dedicated re-engage screen. The research, the plan and the screenshots
live in [`docs/case-studies/issue-31/`](./case-studies/issue-31/README.md).

| ID    | Requirement                                                                                                     | State                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ----- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R-X1  | Find every personal (1:1) contact with no message in either direction for at least N days, 90 by default.       | Done: `findDormantContacts` in `js/src/crm/reengage.js` groups messages by chat, keeps personal chats only (adapter `chatType`, VK peer ranges, or exactly one other participant), skips groups, channels and bots, and by default skips chats where the owner never wrote. The `silentDays` condition of an automation uses it. Tests: `js/tests/reengage.test.js`, `js/tests/automations.test.js`.              |
| R-X2  | The two texts from the issue are the defaults, and both can be replaced.                                        | Done: they are the templates of the `reengage-dormant` preset (`defaultReengageTemplates`); every automation has its own editable informal and formal texts with `{name}`, `{days}` and `{network}`.                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| R-X3  | Detect whether "ты" or "вы" was used more in the dialog with regex rules.                                       | Done: `js/src/crm/address-form.js` — Unicode-aware whole-word markers (pronouns, possessives, verb forms, imperatives, greetings) for Russian, plus 您/你 and आप/तुम; the owner's own messages weigh 2×; the larger score wins. Tests: `js/tests/address-form.test.js`.                                                                                                                                           |
| R-X4  | The detection rules are extensible.                                                                             | Done: `rules` option, and `pattern:*` links from the Patterns screen labelled `address:informal` / `address:formal` become extra rules (`addressRulesFromPatterns`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| R-X5  | The detected form is explainable and has a safe default.                                                        | Done: every draft carries its form and the reason (`majority`/`tie`/`no-evidence`); with no or equal evidence the polite "вы" text is used. The draft card, the preview and `automations review` show the form and let the user switch it.                                                                                                                                                                        |
| R-X6  | Safe (debug) mode is the default: one prefilled message at a time, sent only after the user confirms it.        | Done: an automation in `safe` mode (the default) only queues `automation-draft:*` links; each is sent from its chat's draft card or from `automations review` one at a time, and the server refuses a draft send without `confirm: true`. Tests: `js/tests/automation.test.js`, `js/tests/automations-server.test.js`.                                                                                            |
| R-X7  | Outreach is a configurable automation, not a dedicated screen or chat.                                          | Done: `js/src/automation/automations.js` — trigger → audience → conditions → action, after the triggers of konard/vk-bot; issue #31 is the `reengage-dormant` preset, next to job search, contract search and greet-back. The Automation tab edits, previews and runs them (`js/src/web/automations-view.js`). Tests: `js/tests/automations.test.js`, `js/tests/automations-view.test.js`.                        |
| R-X8  | Automations run by hand, on a schedule, on an incoming message, or from a chat's menu.                          | Done: trigger kinds `manual`, `schedule` (`every: "1d"`), `incoming` (a regex) and `chat-menu`; the scheduler starts with the server (`createAutomationScheduler`); every chat's header has an Automations menu with "Test in this chat" (conditions ignored) and "Run with its conditions".                                                                                                                      |
| R-X9  | The same automations in the terminal and over HTTP.                                                             | Done: `meta-sovereign automations` with `list`, `plan <id>`, `run <id>`, `review`, `drafts`, `history`, `save` and `delete` (`js/src/cli/automation-commands.js`); `/api/automations`, `/api/automations/{plan,run,drafts,history}` and `/api/automations/drafts/{send,form,mark,skip}` (`js/src/server/routes-automations.js`). Tests: `js/tests/automation-cli.test.js`, `js/tests/automations-server.test.js`. |
| R-X10 | Nobody is messaged twice by accident.                                                                           | Done: each run writes an `automation-run:<automation>:<source>:<chat>` record (`sent`, `skipped` or `failed`) and every queued draft is an `automation-draft:*` link; the `cooldownDays` condition and pending drafts leave those chats out, and the preview says why each person was left out.                                                                                                                   |
| R-X11 | A message can be sent by hand when no adapter can send it.                                                      | Done: `prefillLinkFor` gives `mailto:` with the body filled in (RFC 6068), `tg://user?id=` and `https://vk.com/im?sel=`; the draft card's "Open chat" uses it and "I sent it myself" records the send.                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| R-X12 | Automations can run on searchable, cross-referenced people.                                                     | Done: `js/src/crm/people.js` folds messages, `person:`, `member:` and `list:` links into one entry per network account with `sameAs` cross references; `/api/people` searches them; the Automation tab selects people, runs on them or saves them as a list. Tests: `js/tests/people.test.js`.                                                                                                                    |
| R-X13 | Audiences combine friends, chats, communities and lists with union, intersection and difference.                | Done: `resolveAudience` and `parseAudienceSpec` in `js/src/crm/people.js` — specs such as `friends:vk & chat:vk:2000000001 - list:blocked`, saved as `audience:*` links and resolved with a count; VK friends, chat and community members (and the Telegram chat members the Bot API exposes) are synced as `member:` links. Tests: `js/tests/people.test.js`.                                                    |
| R-X14 | Autocomplete from message-to-message reply links, sorted by frequency, then word by word from our own messages. | Done: `js/src/replies/reply-graph.js` learns prompt → reply edges from the archive and in real time; `/api/suggest` answers whole deduplicated replies to the chat's last incoming message by use count, then word-by-word continuations from the owner's messages; the composer shows them as chips (Tab takes the first). Tests: `js/tests/reply-graph.test.js`, `js/tests/reply-service.test.js`.              |
| R-X15 | Use link-cli for fast reply lookups.                                                                            | Done: `js/src/replies/reply-store-clink.js` mirrors the reply graph into `clink --serve` (`MS_REPLY_CLINK=1`) and never blocks a keystroke; lookup costs are measured by `experiments/clink-*.mjs`. Tests: `js/tests/reply-store-clink.test.js`.                                                                                                                                                                  |
| R-X16 | Storage, import and export use the associative stack: Links Notation text and link-cli binary doublets.         | Done: `js/src/storage/doublets-archive.js` writes the link-cli store archive layout, so the same file imports into `clink`; `js/src/storage/lino-store.js` writes one Links Notation record per line. Tests: `js/tests/doublets-archive.test.js`.                                                                                                                                                                 |
| R-X17 | Typing status in every messenger, for people and for automations.                                               | Done: VK `messages.setActivity` and long-poll typing events, Telegram `sendChatAction`, WhatsApp typing indicators and the web UIs; `typing:*` links expire; the chat header shows who is typing, including an automation typing for the user, and our typing is sent while the user types. Tests: `js/tests/live-links.test.js`, `js/tests/chat-view.test.js`.                                                   |
| R-X18 | When an API cannot send, send through the official web UI with browser-commander.                               | Done: `js/src/sources/browser-sender.js` — API first, then vk.com, web.telegram.org or web.whatsapp.com driven by browser-commander with real key events; a signed-out UI is reported as `login-required`. Tests: `js/tests/browser-sender.test.js`.                                                                                                                                                              |
| R-X19 | The chat looks like t3.codes by default, with a Telegram-like option, in React.                                 | Done: `js/src/web/chat-view.js` — searchable sidebar grouped by date, centred message column, floating composer; Settings → Appearance switches skins (t3, Telegram, WhatsApp, Signal) in `js/src/web/appearance.js` and `app.css`. Tests: `js/tests/chat-view.test.js`, `js/tests/appearance.test.js`.                                                                                                           |
| R-X20 | Themes: system, light, dark and auto.                                                                           | Done: `resolveTheme` follows the OS (`prefers-color-scheme`), a fixed choice, or the hour (light 07:00–19:00); the header button still flips light/dark. Tests: `js/tests/appearance.test.js`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
